37H. Synchronization and Queue Policy

 Use synchronization2 semantics and explicit producer/consumer stage/access pairs. "All commands / all
     memory" barriers are diagnostic fallbacks, not production defaults.

 Batch compatible barriers and track resource state centrally. Per-resource barriers are used where
     required for image layouts, ownership transfers or narrower correctness; global memory barriers may
     be better for multiple buffers when the dependency is identical.

 Timeline semaphores coordinate long-lived upload/compute/graphics progress. Binary semaphores
     remain for presentation where required.

 One graphics-capable queue is the correctness baseline. Dedicated compute or transfer queues are
     enabled only when the physical device exposes them and a capture proves useful overlap after
     ownership-transfer and scheduling costs.

 No host wait in the frame-critical path except bounded swapchain/recovery conditions. Readbacks use
     delayed staging and explicit latency.

 Validation, GPU-assisted validation and synchronization validation are mandatory in appropriate
     developer/CI configurations; performance builds may disable them but preserve markers and
     timestamps.

37I. Frame/Render Graph Extraction Rule

 The renderer begins with explicit pass functions plus a tested resource-state tracker and visible
     barriers.

                                         VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 56
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

 A render graph is extracted only after at least three production passes exhibit repeated lifetime aliasing,
     scheduling or synchronization logic. The first graph solves observed duplication; it is not a general-
     purpose DSL.

 Pass declarations include reads, writes, usage class, queue preference, dimensions and debug name.
     Hidden global resource access is prohibited.

 Transient aliasing is disabled until resource lifetime visualization and validation are trustworthy.
 The graph compiler emits a human-readable plan and can run in a conservative no-alias/no-reorder

     mode for debugging.

37J. Shader and Pipeline Reproducibility

 HLSL + pinned DXC remains the production baseline. Compiler version, flags, entry point, target
     environment, defines, include hashes and source hash form the shader cache key.

 SPIR-V is validated. Reflection output is versioned and checked against C++/generated GPU structure
     definitions. Layout tests include size, alignment, offsets and scalar/vector packing.

 Developer hot reload may replace pipelines only after successful compile/validation. A failed reload
     leaves the last valid pipeline active and reports the full diagnostic.

 Pipeline caches are tagged by device/driver and never treated as portable authoritative assets.
 Shader permutations require a manifest and measured need. Runtime string-built permutations,

     unbounded define combinations and silent fallback shaders are prohibited.
 Slang remains a scheduled comparison. It may replace or complement DXC only through an ADR

     covering output correctness, debugging, build determinism, language/tool migration and contributor
     accessibility.

37K. Visual Quality Contract for the Founder North Star

The renderer must support the dense, warm, readable, faceted fantasy look represented by the founder
moodboard without hard-coding one game. Quality is judged by material readability, silhouette stability,
vegetation density, atmospheric depth, contact grounding and clear emissive action effects - not by
photorealism or the highest count of advanced features.

 Linear HDR lighting, exposure control and a documented tone mapper are foundational. Color
     grading/LUT support is configurable art-direction data.

 The first lit path supports sun, sky/ambient contribution, shadowed opaque PBR materials and debug
     views for albedo, normal, roughness, emissive, depth and lighting.

 Voxel surfaces require stable face/derived normals, material palette variation and contact/ambient
     shading that reveals small-scale form without crushing forest shadow detail.

 Vegetation uses hierarchical culling and LOD/impostor strategies. Fully mutable leaf voxels are not a
     requirement; the composition must retain dense canopies and readable motion.

 Magic, fire, debris and gathering impacts may use emissive voxel-like particles, ribbons and meshes.
     They remain transient effects unless gameplay requires persistent world edits.

 Anti-aliasing is selected by captured edge stability and motion. FXAA/SMAA/MSAA/TAA/temporal
     upscaling are candidates; TAA is not accepted before motion vectors, disocclusion handling and
     ghosting tests on voxel edges, foliage and particles.

 Fog and atmospheric perspective are introduced after depth and exposure are stable because they are
     essential to large-vista readability; volumetric clouds and GI remain later quality tiers.

37L. Cross-Vendor and Performance Evidence

 The RTX 4080 Super is the primary development/high-tier device, not sufficient evidence for a
     mandatory engine path.

                                                                                   VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 57
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

 Before an advanced path becomes default, it MUST pass at least one recent AMD GPU and one Intel or
     lower-tier NVIDIA/AMD configuration, whether through local access, trusted contributor captures or a
     reproducible external test packet.

 Every report includes resolution, quality tier, camera path, scene hash, driver, GPU/CPU, selected
     Vulkan profile, warm-up, sample count and frame-time percentiles.

 Record GPU milliseconds per pass, CPU render-thread time, upload bytes, resident/committed VRAM,
     visible/candidate bricks, primitive count, indirect count, barrier count, queue waits, shader/pipeline
     misses and edit-to-visible latency.

 A feature wins only on end-to-end value. A faster GPU pass that increases streaming latency, memory
     spikes, stutter, implementation risk or visual instability may be rejected.

Part VII Acceptance Test

1. The renderer can run the R1 CPU-reference path with validation clean and capture labels intact.
2. A machine-readable engine capability profile selects paths and reports every fallback.

                             VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 58
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution
3. Advanced binding, GPU culling, mesh shaders or ray traversal cannot become mandatory from a single
NVIDIA result.
4. Synchronization dependencies are visible and validated; disabling async queues cannot change
correctness.
5. Every derived GPU record is tied to a source generation and stale uploads cannot replace newer edits.
6. The benchmark packet reports end-to-end edit-to-visible latency, not only isolated render milliseconds.
7. The forest, fortress, magic-effect and large-vista validation scenes preserve readable faceted form, contact
grounding and stable motion at the declared tier.

Part VIII - Streaming, Persistence and Large Coordinates

Part VIII authority contract: Persistent world identity and edits MUST remain independent from camera-
relative rendering, local physics frames and transient residency. Streaming may delay a consumer, but it
may never silently redefine or discard authoritative world state. Parts II, VI and VII remain controlling
context.

38. Research Verdict: Separate Authority from Working Precision

Large-world engineering is not solved by changing every vector from float to double. Epic documents a
mixed model: double-capable engine data, while Niagara retains float data inside world grid cells for
performance. Godot likewise documents the precision benefits and memory/performance cost of global
double precision, and warns that origin shifting becomes especially complex in multiplayer. Genesis
therefore keeps the existing hypothesis: exact or canonical persistent coordinates, plus bounded local
floating-point frames for rendering and simulation. [R76-R77]

This is an engineering hypothesis, not a permanent dogma. Hermes MUST compare it against a global-
double prototype before freezing public coordinate APIs. The comparison includes precision, CPU cost,
memory footprint, shader complexity, physics integration, networking, editor usability and migration cost.

38A. Coordinate Type Taxonomy

     integers, doubles and local floats is forbidden.

 All coordinate arithmetic MUST define overflow behavior. Checked arithmetic is mandatory at file,
     network, procedural-generation and editor boundaries.

 Negative coordinates MUST use one canonical floor-division and modulo rule. Unit tests cover values
     immediately around zero, region edges and integer limits.

 Persistent IDs are never based on memory addresses or local-frame floats.

38B. Units, Resolution and Scale Contract

The smallest authoritative voxel size is a versioned world-format property, not a global compile-time
assumption. A "micro-voxel" describes the engine's ability to represent fine, editable spatial samples where
justified; it does not require uniform maximum resolution across an entire planet.

 Store voxel scale as an exact rational or explicitly versioned conversion, such as integer micrometres
     per voxel or numerator/denominator units. Do not rely on a binary float in the save-file header as the
     only definition.

 Procedural generators, importers, physics proxies and networking MUST declare the coordinate unit
     they consume and produce.

 World-format migration MUST reject a silent voxel-scale change. Conversion requires an offline
     migration or a new world identity.

                                         VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 60
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution
 The Foundation does not claim planetary scale. It proves that persistent addressing and local frames do

     not impose a near-term artificial limit.

38C. Precision Budget

Single-precision floats represent every integer exactly only through +/-16,777,216; practical rendering and
physics tolerance becomes unacceptable much earlier depending on gameplay scale. Godot's published
ranges show that requirements vary by first-person, third-person and top-down use, and that double
precision carries a real cost. Genesis therefore defines precision by measured error budgets rather than a
universal distance claim. [R77]

Consumer                          Error budget to define              Required experiment

First-person camera and tool ray  Maximum visible jitter and          Sweep local-frame radius,
                                  ray/voxel selection error in        camera speed and FOV; record
                                  screen pixels and voxel fractions.  jitter and selection misses.

Character physics                 Maximum contact, grounding          Repeat deterministic traversal
                                  and step error.                     near frame boundary
                                                                      before/after rebasing.

Voxel edits                       Exact selected voxel/brush          Round-trip global -> local ->
                                  footprint.                          ray/edit -> global across negative
                                                                      and boundary coordinates.

Particles and magic               Trail continuity and emitter        Cross-origin stress scene derived
                                  stability.                          from the Part II-A magic
                                                                      moodboard.

Network replication               Zero persistent-coordinate drift.   Serialize, delta, replay and
                                                                      compare canonical integer state
                                                                      across clients.

Editor gizmos                     Selection and transform stability Long-distance edit session with

                                  at distant coordinates.             undo/redo and save/reload.

38D. Local Frames and Origin Rebasing Protocol

Rendering, physics, audio and effects MAY use different local frames when their precision and update
requirements differ. They MUST map through explicit frame objects to the same persistent coordinates. A
rebase is a versioned state transition, not a hidden subtraction applied to arbitrary objects.

54. Select a new origin only at a safe synchronization point after current-frame producers have stopped
     publishing local-space work.

55. Publish a RebasePlan containing old origin, new origin, exact integer delta, affected consumers and
     target frame generation.

56. Cancel or retarget in-flight derived jobs whose input generation or frame generation no longer matches.
57. Apply the delta to bounded local state: physics bodies, camera-relative transforms, audio emitters,

     particle systems and debug tools.
58. Rebuild or invalidate any cache whose local coordinates cannot be transformed exactly.

                                  VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 61
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

59. Run post-rebase invariants: persistent hashes unchanged, local bounds valid, camera/tool continuity
     within the declared error budget.

60. Record the event in profiling and replay diagnostics. Do not write a camera-origin rebase as a world
     edit.

  Multiplayer rule: Servers and persistent storage never rebase their authority. Each client may choose its
  own local origin. Network messages carry canonical coordinates or region/local integer tuples; local
  floats remain a presentation detail.

39. Streaming Is a Multi-Consumer Residency Service

Streaming owns movement of authoritative and derived data between storage, CPU memory and GPU
memory. It does not own the logical world. Render visibility is one demand source among several; an
invisible support surface may still be mandatory for physics, editing, authority or network interest.

39A. Canonical State Machine

Absent

 Every request, authority object and derived object has a generation. A late job cannot publish over a
     newer edit or newer local frame.

 Transitions MUST be idempotent. Replaying a completion or cancellation cannot double-free, double-
     publish or lose a lease.

 Cancellation is cooperative and observable. "Ignored result" is not sufficient when work still consumes
     unbounded I/O, memory or GPU upload budget.

 The state machine distinguishes authoritative readiness from each consumer's readiness. "Loaded"
     alone is not a valid state.

A lease declares consumer, spatial bounds, minimum representation, priority class, deadline,
expiration/cancellation token and estimated byte/work cost. Multiple leases coalesce without erasing the
strictest requirement. A consumer MUST release leases explicitly or through scoped lifetime.

39C. Priority, Hysteresis and Hierarchical Detail

Render priority SHOULD use geometric error, screen-space error, distance, visibility and motion prediction
as inputs. The 3D Tiles specification demonstrates a mature HLOD selection model using bounding volumes
and screen-space error; Genesis adopts the principle, not the file format. Physics, edit and authority
demands can override the render metric. [R78]

 Use separate enter and exit thresholds to prevent LOD and residency thrashing.
 Prediction MUST be bounded by measured accuracy. Record bytes loaded but never consumed,

     cancelled work and false predictions.
 A priority score MUST remain explainable in debug UI. Hidden machine-learned priority is prohibited

     in Foundation.
 Far-field quality may degrade before physics or edit correctness. The system may not trade player

     grounding for prettier distant terrain.

No unbounded queues: Every queue has a byte cap, item cap, age metric and overload behavior. A
queue may reject, merge, degrade or backpressure; it may not grow until the process or GPU fails.

39E. Safe Eviction

61. Mark a candidate only when no hard lease remains and no non-cancellable consumer is publishing into
     it.

62. Ensure dirty authority is committed to the declared durability level or is reconstructible from an
     already durable transaction.

63. Invalidate handles by generation before freeing storage; late users must fail closed.
64. Evict derived data before authoritative data unless the authority is reproducible and the derived cache

     is demonstrably more valuable.
65. Retain tombstone/metrics long enough to diagnose thrashing and stale access.
66. Stress-test rapid enter/exit loops, teleportation, camera cuts, disconnects and edit bursts.

40. Persistence Contract: Base + Transaction Journal + Immutable Checkpoints

SQLite's atomic-commit and WAL documentation is used as design evidence: durable commits require an
explicit commit boundary, careful ordering and real flush semantics; appending a log is not automatically
crash safe. SQLite also shows that checkpoints are a workload-dependent policy and that a WAL generally
has one writer. VoxelForge does not copy SQLite's format, but MUST meet equivalent, tested invariants for
its declared durability profile. [R79-R80]

                                   VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 65
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

40A. Durability Profiles

Profile                Acknowledgement point   Allowed loss after power  Initial use
                                               failure

D0 - Disposable        Authority exists only in Entire session.          Benchmarks, generated
                       memory.                                           caches, temporary
                                                                         previews.

D1 - Session recovery  Transaction appended    Documented recent         Editor autosave option
                       and process-visible,    window; OS/power loss     and local rapid iteration.
                       without forced durable  may exceed process-
                       flush.                  crash guarantee.

                       required data flushed   committed transaction,    and release tests.
                       according to platform   subject to documented
                       contract.               hardware/filesystem
                                               limits.

                       replica/quorum          disaster-recovery plan.
                       acknowledgement.

 The active profile is visible in UI/logs and part of every save/benchmark record.
 Hermes may not claim "crash safe" without power-cut/fault-injection evidence for the exact platform

     and profile.
 Changing durability to improve performance is a product/policy decision, not a hidden optimization.

40B. Transaction and Journal Record

The minimum atomic unit is a versioned world transaction: command/tick identity, affected canonical
coordinates, preconditions or source generations, deterministic operation payload, content checksum,
schema version and commit marker. Partial records are ignored during recovery; a commit is visible only
when all required records and the commit marker validate.

 Journal segments are append-only while active and have bounded maximum size.
 Checksums detect accidental corruption; a cryptographic content hash may identify immutable

     snapshots and provenance but is not a substitute for an adversarial security design.
 The writer serializes transactions per authority shard/region in Foundation. Multiple concurrent

     writers require a later protocol and benchmark.
 Edits that span several regions are split into deterministic subtransactions unless an explicit

     coordinator record and recovery protocol are implemented.
 Acknowledgement is decoupled from visual rebuild. A committed edit may still have stale derived

     meshes for a bounded interval.

40C. Checkpointing and Compaction

67. Select a stable authority generation without stopping unrelated reads. Use versioned/copy-on-write
     input or a short bounded capture phase.

                                               VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 66
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

68. Write a new immutable checkpoint to a temporary identity with header, block index, schema,
     world/generator version, sizes and hashes.

69. Flush according to the selected durability profile.
70. Publish the new checkpoint through an atomic manifest/version switch supported by the target

     filesystem contract.
71. Retain the previous known-good checkpoint and journal until the new manifest has been reopened and

     verified.
72. Truncate/archive old journal segments only after recovery testing proves the new checkpoint is

     sufficient.
73. Schedule checkpoints from journal size, replay cost, dirty pressure and frame/service load - not a fixed

     magic interval.

40D. Crash Recovery Algorithm

74. Open the newest manifest whose structure, checksum and referenced files validate.
75. Load the immutable checkpoint with strict allocation and size limits.
76. Scan journal segments in deterministic order. Stop at the first incomplete or invalid transaction

     boundary; never guess missing bytes.
77. Apply only committed transactions whose schema and preconditions are understood.
78. Recompute authority hashes and invariants for touched regions.
79. Quarantine corrupt later segments and produce a human-readable recovery report.
80. Rebuild all render, collision, navigation and GPU caches from authority; never restore them as

     unquestioned truth.

40E. Procedural Base and Generator Versioning

A world seed is insufficient for reproducibility. The format records generator identity, semantic version,
configuration hash, material registry version and deterministic random-stream contract. When a generator
changes, the engine MUST choose one of three explicit policies: retain the old generator module, bake
affected base data into a checkpoint, or run an offline migration. Silent regeneration under a new algorithm
is forbidden.

41. File and Block Format Requirements

                   generator/material versions; active checkpoint and
                   journal set; durability metadata; checksum/hash.

 Readers validate every offset, multiplication, decompressed size and allocation against configured limits
     before use.

 Released schemas require migration fixtures and a retained corpus. "We can delete old worlds" is not
     an acceptable default after a public release.

 Fuzzing targets headers, indexes, codecs, journal replay and migration. Malformed files must fail safely
     without unbounded memory or path traversal.

 Region/pack file size is selected from measured I/O, update locality, backup and repair behavior; one file
     per tiny brick is prohibited unless evidence overturns the metadata/fragmentation cost.

42. Compression Selection Protocol

Compression is selected on VoxelForge brick corpora, not generic benchmark charts. Zstandard is a strong
checkpoint/journal candidate because its format is stable and its dictionary mode targets families of small
correlated records; LZ4 is a strong low-latency decode candidate. Raw/uncompressed storage remains a
required reference. [R82-R83]

 A codec cannot define the authority format unless a migration/fallback story exists.

43. I/O Architecture

Foundation uses a portable asynchronous abstraction over bounded worker-based or native-overlapped
reads. The API expresses offsets, byte ranges, priority, cancellation, destination/staging ownership and
completion. It does not expose a Windows-only feature to world-format or gameplay layers.

 Coalesce adjacent reads only when saved request overhead exceeds read amplification.
 Separate I/O completion from decompression and authority publication; each stage has independent

     budgets and traces.
 Do not hold world locks while waiting for disk, decompression or GPU upload.
 Use buffer pools only after measuring allocation/copy overhead. Ownership and cancellation must

     remain explicit.
 Simulate slow disks, partial reads, disk-full, permission errors, truncated files and delayed completions

     in automated tests.

43A. DirectStorage Decision Gate

Microsoft positions DirectStorage for high-throughput NVMe workloads with many small reads and reduced
CPU overhead, including compressed-asset paths. It is a relevant Windows experiment, not the Foundation
baseline. [R81]

Requirement before admission     Evidence

Portable path already correct    Same world and scene load through baseline I/O
                                 with identical authority hash.

Real bottleneck identified       Trace shows storage submission/decompression
                                 CPU is material in disk-to-ready latency.

End-to-end win                   Improves P95/P99 time-to-authority or time-to-
                                 visible, not only synthetic read throughput.

GPU budget protected             GPU decompression/upload does not create
                                 unacceptable rendering or compute contention.

Fallback and file compatibility  World files remain readable through the portable
                                 path; feature failure degrades cleanly.

Maintenance justified            Windows-specific code, packaging, debugging and
                                 driver matrix fit current milestone budget.

Part VIII Acceptance Test

81. Persistent coordinate round-trips are exact across negative coordinates, region edges, rebase events and
     save/network serialization.

82. A global-double comparison prototype and the integer-global/local-float prototype produce documented
     precision/performance results before public coordinate APIs freeze.

                                 VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 69
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

83. Teleport, camera-cut and high-speed traversal tests cannot create unbounded queues, stale publication
     or missing physics support.

84. Every resident object has visible leases, generation, byte cost, state and cancellation/eviction reason.
85. Power-cut/fault-injection recovery yields either the last acknowledged transaction for the selected

     durability profile or a documented earlier state; corruption is reported, never silently accepted.
86. Checkpointing cannot lose concurrent committed edits, and journal replay time is measured at

     P50/P95/P99.
87. Compression is chosen from project corpora with raw and at least one fast and one ratio-oriented

     comparison.
88. DirectStorage or any platform-specific fast path remains optional and bit-for-bit authority compatible

     with the portable path.
89. The Part II-A forest, fortress, destruction and magic scenes can stream while preserving tactile edits,

     stable effects and bounded edit-to-visible latency.

Part IX - CPU, Memory and Concurrency

  Part IX prime rule: Correct ownership and bounded work precede clever scheduling. Standard C++
  facilities are the baseline. Custom allocation, lock-free structures, fibers, coroutines or an ECS become
  production dependencies only after a representative trace and an ADR show that the simpler path is
  insufficient.

44. C++ Runtime and Ownership Policy

The C++ Core Guidelines treat memory, file handles, sockets and locks as resources with explicit owners and
recommend RAII; they also prefer unique ownership unless sharing is the actual model. Genesis adopts
those principles as the Foundation baseline. [R84]
 Every resource has one clear owner or a documented shared-lifetime model. Destructors release

     resources and are safe during partial construction/failure.
 std::vector, std::string, std::span, std::array, std::unique_ptr, scoped locks and value types are admitted

     defaults.
 Raw pointers/references express non-owning access only. Public APIs document validity, mutability and

     thread requirements.
 std::shared_ptr is prohibited as a convenience lifetime patch in hot or authority-critical code. Admission

     requires a real shared-ownership graph and cycle strategy.
 Global mutable state is prohibited except narrow process services approved by ADR and resettable in

     tests.
 Handles crossing asynchronous, persistence or GPU boundaries include generation/version validation.

44A. Error and Exception Boundaries

One project-wide exception policy MUST be accepted before production modules diverge. The initial
hypothesis is: tools/importers may use exceptions internally; frame-critical runtime, C ABI, job and thread
boundaries convert failures to explicit status/result objects and never allow an exception to escape a worker
or callback boundary.
 Constructors either establish a valid RAII object or fail without leaking; partially valid hidden states are

     avoided.
 Fatal programmer invariants use assertions in development and structured crash evidence.

     Recoverable content/I/O failures use explicit errors.

                                                                                   VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 70
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

 Error messages carry operation, canonical resource identity, platform code and causal context without
     exposing secrets.

 Ignoring a returned error requires an explicit helper or annotation and review; discarded status by
     accident is a build/lint defect.

 No exception/no-exception build-mode split may change serialized or network behavior.

44B. ABI, Types and Boundaries

 Do not expose STL containers, exceptions or compiler-specific layout across plugin/C ABI boundaries.
     Use versioned C-compatible descriptors or opaque handles where binary stability is required.

 Strong unit/coordinate/ID types prevent accidental mixing. Arithmetic narrowing is explicit and
     checked at authority boundaries.

 Public subsystem interfaces remain smaller than implementations. Do not create an abstract interface
     merely because replacement is imaginable; create one when a tested boundary needs independent
     evolution.

 Templates and compile-time machinery must justify build-time, diagnostics and binary-size cost.

45. Allocation Policy: Observe First, Specialize Second

Foundation begins with the standard allocator for ordinary CPU ownership and VMA for Vulkan allocation.
Instrument allocation count, bytes, lifetime, thread and call site before introducing custom allocators. The
goal is predictable lifetime and pressure behavior, not an "allocator empire."

Lifetime class   Baseline representation           Specialization gate

                 owners                            default.

Frame transient  standard containers               Linear/frame arena only after
                 reused/reserved                   allocation/copy trace proves
                                                   material cost.

Task scratch     caller-owned buffer or scoped     Arena/slab after task
                 container                         concurrency and peak demand
                                                   are measured.

                 staging lifetime                  allocations based on resource-

                                                       after platform experiment.

45A. Allocation and Memory Budgets

 Every major subsystem reports current, peak, reserved/committed and evictable bytes.
 Budgets use soft and hard watermarks. The soft watermark triggers degradation/eviction; the hard

     watermark rejects or backpressures new work before process failure.
 Memory accounting includes allocator overhead, compressed caches, staging, journal buffers and

     derived copies - not only payload arrays.
 A reusable buffer is not automatically a permanent cache. Retention policy is tied to measured reuse

     and pressure.
 Out-of-memory paths are fault-injected. Authority writes fail explicitly or backpressure; they do not

     continue with partially mutated state.

45B. Custom Allocator Admission Gate

Question                      Required evidence

Is allocation material?       Trace shows significant frame/service time,
                              contention, fragmentation, peak memory or latency
                              tail.

Is lifetime regular?          Objects share a demonstrable lifetime/size pattern
                              suitable for arena, slab or pool.

Can standard reuse solve it?  reserve/reuse/batching/SoA alternatives were
                              measured and insufficient.

Can failure be tested?        Exhaustion, reset, cancellation and owner
                              teardown have automated tests.

Is instrumentation retained?  Special allocator reports at least equivalent
                              ownership, bytes and call-site evidence.

Can it be removed?            Boundary and benchmark permit reverting to the
                              standard path.

46. Concurrency Baseline: Bounded Tasks, Not Autonomous Threads

The C++ Core Guidelines recommend reasoning in tasks rather than threads and minimizing shared
writable data. Genesis starts with a small bounded executor and explicit task groups; it does not begin with
fibers, a universal task graph, lock-free containers or a worker per subsystem. [R84]

                              VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 72
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

46A. Initial Thread Roles

Role                          Initial responsibility              Must not do

Main/orchestration            input, frame/service phases, high-  Long I/O, shader compilation,
                              level publication                   decompression or arbitrary
                                                                  blocking.

Render submission             Vulkan command                      Own world authority or wait on
                              preparation/submission if           unrelated background work.
                              profiling justifies separation

CPU worker pool               bounded compute tasks: decode, Unbounded blocking I/O or

                              mesh, collision, generation         detached lifetime.

I/O completion/blocking pool  portable/native reads, waits and    Heavy compute that starves
                              completion translation              completions.

Persistence writer per shard  serialize ordered authority         Multiple unsynchronized writers
                              transactions/checkpoints            to one journal.

External tool/model process   separate process with timeout       Run inside frame-critical worker
                              and sandbox                         or hold engine locks.

Worker count is calibrated per machine and workload. Hardware thread count is an input, not the answer.
The scheduler reserves capacity for OS, driver, I/O and render requirements and records oversubscription.

46B. Task Contract

 Task inputs are immutable snapshots, value copies, versioned handles or explicitly synchronized
     ownership.

 Each task declares owner/group, priority class, cancellation token, source generation and
     completion/publication path.

 No detached task may outlive its subsystem, plugin, world or benchmark fixture.
 Task completion does not imply publication. Publication rechecks generation, cancellation and

     destination lifetime.
 Dependencies describe data readiness, not arbitrary sequencing. A task graph that mirrors every

     function call is rejected.
 Blocking waits inside CPU workers are prohibited except a documented short bounded primitive. Long

     waits move to I/O/blocking infrastructure.

46C. Task Granularity Calibration

There is no universal microsecond target. Hermes MUST benchmark enqueue, wake, steal, completion and
dependency overhead on the target CPU and under contention. A batch should usually contain comfortably
more useful work than scheduler overhead, while still preserving cancellation and load balance.

                                                      VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 73
Benchmark         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution
                                                                        Output

Empty/no-op tasks                lower-bound scheduling overhead and queue
                                 contention.

Uniform CPU kernels              throughput/latency across batch sizes and worker
                                 counts.

Mixed long/short tasks           tail latency, starvation and fairness.

Cancellation storm               time/bytes wasted after demand disappears.

Brick edit burst                 authority lock time, stale jobs, edit-to-visible
                                 latency.

Decode + mesh + upload pipeline  cross-stage backpressure and core occupancy.

46D. Queue and Work-Stealing Decision

Start with the simplest bounded queue/executor that meets correctness and observability. Compare a
central MPMC queue with per-worker deques/work stealing only on representative mixed workloads.
Taskflow is a useful research implementation and paper for task-graph concepts, but it is not admitted as a
runtime dependency without the normal dependency and benchmark gate. [R89]

 A work-stealing design must prove better utilization/tails without unacceptable nondeterminism,
     memory overhead or debugging cost.

 Priority queues must prevent starvation. Low-priority checkpoint/prefetch work receives aging or
     reserved budget, not permanent neglect.

 Queue saturation is surfaced and handled; producers never spin indefinitely or silently allocate an
     overflow queue.

47. C++ Memory Model and Synchronization Rules

Under the C++ memory model, conflicting concurrent non-atomic actions without a happens-before
relationship form a data race; a valid engine architecture must make those relationships explicit. Atomics
do not automatically make a compound invariant correct. [R85]

 Default to mutex/scoped-lock or single-writer ownership for compound state. Use atomics for narrow
     independently defined state and queues whose algorithm has been reviewed.

 Sequentially consistent atomics are the initial default. Weaker memory orders require a written
     happens-before argument, targeted stress test and reviewer approval.

 volatile is never a synchronization primitive.
 No lock is held while calling external/user/plugin code, performing disk/network I/O, waiting on GPU

     fences or invoking an AI/tool process.
 Define a lock hierarchy for the few locks that can nest. Debug builds detect ordering violations and long-

     held locks.
 Condition-variable predicates are checked in loops; wakeups do not substitute for state.
 False sharing and cache-line padding are addressed only after profiling and on types where the layout

     cost is justified.

                                 VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 74
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

47A. Mutable Brick Ownership

Foundation uses one logical writer at a time per mutable brick or authority shard. Readers consume
immutable snapshots/versioned views or hold a short explicit read guard. The edit transaction publishes a
new generation; derived jobs never mutate authority.

90. Resolve the canonical brick(s) and acquire transaction ownership in deterministic spatial order.
91. Validate expected generation/preconditions.
92. Apply the edit to private or exclusively owned mutable state.
93. Append/commit the persistence transaction according to Part VIII policy.
94. Publish the new authority generation atomically at the subsystem boundary.
95. Schedule derived rebuilds using immutable inputs and the published generation.
96. Release ownership before any GPU, disk or external wait.

  Lock-free warning: A lock-free algorithm is not automatically lower latency, simpler or safer. It is
  forbidden in authority/persistence paths until contention data, progress guarantees, ABA/lifetime
  handling and sanitizer/stress evidence are documented in an ADR.

48. Determinism and Reproducible Simulation

Determinism is an explicit property of selected systems, not a promise that every task executes in the same
order. The scheduler may be nondeterministic while authoritative command ordering, procedural
generation and persistence remain reproducible.

 Authoritative commands have a canonical order: server/tick sequence plus deterministic tie-break
     rules.

 Randomness uses named, versioned streams with recorded seeds; task execution order never selects
     random values implicitly.

 Parallel reductions define ordering or use numerically stable/quantized algorithms where exact replay
     matters.

 Unordered container iteration cannot affect authority, serialization hashes or network-visible outcomes
     unless explicitly canonicalized.

 Replay tests hash authoritative brick/entity state at checkpoints and report the first divergence with
     command and generation context.

 Rendering, particles and non-authoritative ambience may be nondeterministic if it cannot feed back
     into authority.

49. ECS and Data-Oriented Architecture Decision Gate

Data-oriented layout is a method, not a requirement to install a universal ECS. Early voxel authority,
streaming and rendering should use domain-specific arrays/handles. When representative gameplay
entities exist, implement two isolated spikes: plain SoA/handles and one mature ECS candidate. Compare
performance and engineering cost before accepting an ECS through ADR.

Evaluation dimension         Required evidence

Iteration/query performance  Representative entity/component distributions;
Structural changes           P50/P95 update cost.

                             Spawn/despawn/archetype churn and
                             synchronization with networking/editor.

                             VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 75
               MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

Evaluation dimension                       Required evidence

Serialization and persistence              Stable IDs, schema evolution and save/replay
                                           integration.

Scheduling                                 Can systems expose safe task dependencies without
                                           hidden global mutation?

Debugging/tooling                          Inspectability, breakpoints, ownership and editor
                                           integration.

Build and API cost                         Compile time, template diagnostics, binary size and
                                           contributor learning cost.

Boundary discipline                        ECS cannot become the owner of voxel authority or
                                           renderer resources by convenience.

49A. Sanitizer and Dynamic Analysis Matrix

Sanitizers are scheduled test configurations, not optional emergency tools. Clang documents
AddressSanitizer for out-of-bounds/use-after-free/double-free classes and ThreadSanitizer for data races;
Microsoft supports AddressSanitizer on Windows x86/x64 but does not currently provide MSVC
ThreadSanitizer. This makes a Linux/Clang TSan CI lane a constitutional requirement once concurrency
begins. [R86-R88]

 Sanitizer suppressions are narrow, owned, dated and justified; blanket suppression of project code is
     forbidden.

 A sanitizer-clean run does not prove race freedom or correctness. Stress, model invariants and code
     review remain required.

 Performance comparisons never use sanitizer builds.

Part IX Acceptance Test

97. All resources have explicit ownership; no job, callback or plugin can outlive its owner without a
     versioned handle and cancellation path.

98. Foundation builds run with project warnings treated as errors and pass the configured ASan/UBSan
     lanes; concurrency milestones add a passing targeted TSan lane.

99. No unbounded thread creation, detached tasks or blocking disk/GPU/model waits exist in the CPU
     worker pool.

100. Task granularity and worker count are selected from calibration data on the developer machine
     and at least one mainstream CPU profile.

101. Mutable brick edits obey one-writer/versioned-publication rules and survive contention,
     cancellation and shutdown stress.

102. Every non-default atomic memory order and every lock-free structure has a documented proof
     sketch, test and ADR.

                                                                                   VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 77
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

103. Memory pressure causes bounded degradation/backpressure rather than authority corruption or
     process-wide uncontrolled growth.

104. Deterministic replay reproduces authoritative hashes across repeated schedules and reports exact
     divergence when deliberately faulted.

105. An ECS remains absent until the representative comparison packet proves net value.

Part X - Physics, Destruction and Simulation

Part X authority contract: The World Model owns material, occupancy, edit history and persistent object
identity. Physics owns a bounded, replaceable simulation of collision and motion derived from that
authority. A solver result may request or validate a world edit, but it may never silently become a
second persistent truth. Parts VI, VIII and IX control brick versions, coordinates, persistence and
concurrency.

50. Research Verdict: Destruction Is a Versioned World Edit with Bounded Physical
Consequences

VoxelForge must support tactile excavation, construction and destruction in the experiential direction of
the founder moodboard. That goal does not justify simulating every voxel as a rigid body, constraint or
finite element. The production hypothesis is a layered model: authoritative voxel edits; derived collision
proxies; selected rigid bodies; and optional, local structural analysis. High-resolution finite-element fracture
can create convincing deformation and crack propagation, but the cited research also demonstrates a much
heavier simulation model than the Foundation needs. It remains a research tier, not the default runtime.
[R94]

 A destructive action produces an explicit, validated EditTransaction. Visual fragments, sound, particles
     and camera response are consequences, not authority.

 Collision, support graphs, navigation and GPU meshes rebuild from the committed brick generation.
     They may lag within declared budgets but cannot publish stale results.

 The engine targets physically legible and gameplay-consistent destruction, not civil-engineering
     accuracy.

 All simulation tiers must degrade predictably under stress. The correct response to overload is coarser
     proxies, fewer dynamic components or deferred work - never an unbounded body explosion.

50B. Time, Units and Local Physics Frames

 Physics uses SI-like units: metres, kilograms, seconds and radians. The voxel-to-metre conversion is
     explicit and versioned.

 The default simulation is fixed-step. Rendering may interpolate presentation; authoritative edits and
     server ticks use integer tick identifiers.

 Local physics scenes use the Part VIII FrameOrigin contract. Global persistent coordinates are converted
     at a controlled boundary, never scattered through solver callbacks.

 Jolt documents best operating ranges for object sizes/speeds and a mixed double/float big-world path.
     VoxelForge still benchmarks local-frame float and Jolt double-precision modes before freezing the
     adapter. [R91]

 A rebase occurs only at a physics synchronization point. Bodies, queries, cached contacts and debug
     views must observe one frame version per step.

51. Physics Backend Boundary and Jolt Admission

Jolt Physics remains the leading Foundation candidate because it is C++, multithread-oriented, supports
static/dynamic/kinematic bodies, character controllers, breakable constraints, custom shapes, deterministic
modes and rollback state APIs. Admission is still conditional: VoxelForge must wrap it behind a narrow
physics interface and retain its own authoritative formats. Jolt explicitly warns that cooked binary shapes
are version-sensitive and should not be the primary content format. [R91-R93]

 No engine module outside Physics may include Jolt headers except approved adapter/test boundaries.
 Persistent entity and world IDs map to generation-checked PhysicsHandle values; Jolt BodyID is not a

     save-file or network identity.
 The Physics module owns creation/destruction ordering, step synchronization, callbacks and shape

     cooking.

                                                                                   VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 79
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

 Contact and activation callbacks enqueue bounded facts/events; they do not directly edit world
     authority while the solver is updating.

 A fallback query-only reference path is preserved long enough to verify adapter behavior and allow
     headless world tests without the full solver.

The chosen proxy may differ by material, distance and use. Character grounding can use a finer proxy than
sleeping debris; far inactive zones need no live collision. Visual and collision detail may differ only inside an
explicit tolerance and must be shown in a debug overlay.

51B. Dirty Collision Rebuild and Publication Protocol

106. Commit a world EditTransaction and increment affected brick/cluster generations.
107. Mark collision dependency records dirty and enqueue bounded cooking work with the source

     generation.
108. Build proxy data off the simulation step using immutable source snapshots.
109. Validate source generation, local-frame version and residency lease before publication.

                                                 VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 80
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

110. At a physics synchronization point, batch-remove or replace old bodies/shapes and batch-add new
     proxies.

111. Wake intersecting dynamic bodies when removed/replaced collision could invalidate sleep. Jolt
     explicitly provides area activation for this case. [R91]

112. Retire old proxies only after no query/step can access them; record build time, swap time and
     memory.

113. If work is stale or the region was evicted, discard it without publishing side effects.

 Adding many bodies individually is forbidden in streaming paths; Jolt recommends its batch add path
     and notes broadphase degradation from repeated one-by-one insertion. [R91]

 A collision cook failure leaves the previous valid proxy in service where safe, marks the region
     degraded and raises a visible diagnostic. It never publishes an empty collision shape as silent success.

 Edits that remove support under a character or rigid body receive a deterministic correction policy:
     depenetration/ground reacquisition, bounded fall or server correction.

51C. Character Controller Policy

The Foundation compares Jolt CharacterVirtual with a simple kinematic/rigid-body character prototype.
CharacterVirtual is the leading candidate because it exposes explicit movement/collision control without
requiring the avatar to behave like an unconstrained dynamic body. Selection is based on stairs, slopes,
ledges, moving platforms, freshly edited terrain and server reconciliation - not preference. [R93]

 Character movement consumes a stable collision snapshot for one tick and publishes an authoritative
     transform result.

 Tool rays and voxel selection use the World/voxel query path; the character controller does not become
     the source of edit coordinates.

 Ground state, step decisions and contact normals are debug-recorded to reproduce edge cases.
 Client prediction may reuse movement code, but the dedicated server remains authoritative.

51D. Dynamic Bodies, CCD, Sleeping and Material Mapping

 Simple convex primitives and convex hulls are preferred for dynamic objects. Dynamic triangle meshes
     are exceptional because Jolt documents them as expensive and without a clear inside/outside. [R91]

 Continuous collision detection is enabled by measured risk (fast/small bodies), not globally.
 Sleeping is required for scalability. World edits activate only bodies in a conservative affected volume,

     not every body in a region.
 VoxelMaterialID maps through a versioned PhysicsMaterial table containing density/mass policy,

     friction, restitution, damage class and optional sound/effect tags.
 Render material and physics material are related data, not the same object. A visual palette change

     cannot silently alter mass or support behavior.

52. Destruction Command and Damage Semantics

Damage is an input to a policy, not a direct write to occupancy. The policy evaluates actor authority, tool
shape, material resistance, protected ownership, edit budget and server rules, then emits a deterministic
EditTransaction or rejection.

Stage   Required data                         Output

Intent  Actor/session, tool/ability, origin,  Validated DamageIntent or
        shape, tick, client command           rejection reason.
        sequence.

                       VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 81

                                hardness/toughness class,         cost.
                                protection/ownership,

                                and before/after values.          generations, authoritative result
                                                                  hash.

                                                                  work plus bounded presentation
                                                                  effects.

 The server recomputes or bounds the edit footprint. A client-supplied list of voxels is never trusted as
     authority.

 A large brush/explosion is partitioned into bounded transactions with a clear all-or-nothing or
     progressive-commit policy.

 Gameplay damage accumulation may be stored at a coarser layer than every voxel. Per-voxel extra
     channels require a memory and persistence budget.

 Undo/editor operations and gameplay destruction share the same journal invariants but have different
     permissions and retention policies.

52A. Structural Integrity: Tiered Approximation, Not Hidden FEM

Structural collapse is valuable to the desired fantasy sandbox, but it is also an unbounded graph/simulation
problem if every sample participates continuously. Genesis therefore uses staged, local approximations. The
first production tier answers a gameplay question - "is this component still connected to an accepted
support anchor?" - rather than solving continuous stress tensors.

                     analysis.                 and manual edits.          structural destruction.

 Connectivity work is limited to dirty support islands plus a safety halo. A removal cannot trigger an
     unbounded flood fill over the whole world in one tick.

 Deletions make incremental union-find insufficient by itself; the Foundation may use bounded
     relabel/flood-fill of affected islands before considering fully dynamic connectivity algorithms.

 Support anchors are explicit and inspectable: terrain, foundation material, authored anchors or stable
     world boundaries. "Connected to any voxel" is not enough.

 The debug view displays support cells, anchors, island IDs, pending work and the exact reason a
     component detached or remained fixed.

52B. Detached Voxel Components

114. Identify a disconnected occupied component within the bounded structural work area.
115. Reject components below a dust threshold as visual debris or remove/settle them according to

     material policy.
116. Reject or progressively split components above maximum volume/mass/body budgets; never create

     an unbounded dynamic volume in one step.
117. Extract a compact immutable component payload and remove/mark its source voxels in the same

     authoritative transaction.
118. Generate a simplified convex/compound collision representation; detailed surface data remains a

     render cache.
119. Create one or a small bounded set of rigid bodies with stable component IDs and server ownership.
120. On sleep/settle, either keep it as an entity, re-voxelize through an explicit transaction, convert to

     rubble, or expire according to policy.

 Maximum components per event, maximum dynamic voxels per active zone, maximum collision
     triangles/hulls and maximum lifetime are configuration budgets with telemetry.

 Detached-body save/network behavior is explicit. Presentation-only fragments are never serialized as
     authoritative entities.

 Re-voxelization must define overlap resolution, material conservation, orientation sampling and anti-
     duplication guarantees before it is enabled.

                                       VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 83
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

52C. Debris and Presentation Effects

Debris exists in three classes: authoritative rigid components; lightweight server-owned gameplay debris;
and client-only presentation particles/voxel fragments. The class is selected before spawn. Promotion from
presentation to authority is forbidden.

 Presentation effects derived from destruction may be richer on the founder RTX 4080 Super, but
     correctness tests use a deterministic minimal effect mode.

 Particles, dust, sparks, foliage movement, sound and camera shake consume an event record and do not
     query arbitrary mutable world state later.

 Debris budgets are load-shed in a stable order: reduce particles, merge visual fragments, reduce
     gameplay debris, delay new detachments, never discard committed world edits.

52D. Deferred Simulation Channels

Channel                  Foundation status               Admission question

Fluids                   Deferred.                       Can a sparse, bounded regional
                                                         solver add gameplay value
                                                         without permanent per-voxel
                                                         state?

Heat/fire                Event/material prototype only.  What state resolution and tick
                                                         frequency are affordable and
                                                         persistable?

Erosion/weathering       Offline/procedural or coarse    Can results be represented as
                         background model.               normal edit transactions?

Soft bodies/deformation  External/Jolt research.         Is this for selected entities rather
                                                         than world authority?

Surrogate ML physics     Research-only.                  Does it beat a deterministic
                                                         approximation, expose
                                                         uncertainty and fail safely?

53. Simulation Scheduling and Determinism Contract

 The server/world tick orders accepted commands, world commits, dependency scheduling and physics-
     step boundaries. The order is recorded in deterministic replay logs.

 Physics runs at a fixed configured rate with a bounded number of catch-up steps. Overload is visible;
     the server does not enter an unlimited spiral of death.

 Active simulation zones are residency consumers with budgets. Distant sleeping worlds remain
     persisted data, not silently simulated at full fidelity.

 Authoritative voxel edit operations are integer-addressed and deterministic. Rigid-body outcomes need
     not be bit-identical across arbitrary clients because the server is authoritative.

 Jolt documents deterministic execution when API mutation order and binary/configuration are
     controlled, plus a cross-platform deterministic build option with cost and caveats. VoxelForge may use
     this for tests or specific rollback domains, but it does not assume that full MMO state can be
     reconstructed from client physics alone. [R91]

                                            VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 84
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

 Jolt rollback snapshots cover solver-modified state and impose strict requirements when
     bodies/constraints are added or removed. Whole-world rollback is therefore not the Foundation
     networking model. [R91]

53A. Physics Persistence and Network Boundary

 Persist canonical voxel/world state, stable entities and explicit gameplay properties. Treat cooked
     collision shapes, contact caches, broadphase trees and solver islands as disposable caches.

 For persistent rigid entities, save stable transform, velocity, sleep state as needed, shape/material
     references and gameplay state - not raw internal solver memory.

 Network authoritative world edits, entity transforms/velocities and discrete gameplay events. Do not
     replicate contact manifolds or every fragment impulse.

 Client-only debris and prediction state are discarded on reconciliation. Server-owned dynamic voxel
     components use bounded snapshot/state updates and explicit correction.

 A physics backend upgrade requires recooking derived caches and replaying compatibility tests; it must
     not require rewriting the authoritative voxel world.

53B. Required Diagnostics and Failure Injection

 Overlay collision proxies, visual/collision error, body IDs, active/sleeping state, contact counts, support
     islands and pending proxy generations.

 Record per-tick body count, active count, collision pairs, narrowphase time, solver time, cook queues,
     stale-job discards, detached component count and debris load shedding.

 Inject cancelled cooks, failed shape creation, delayed jobs, rapid edits under bodies, origin rebases,
     save/load during sleep and network corrections.

 Maintain deterministic edit/physics scenario files that can be replayed headless in CI and visually in the
     editor.

54. Part X Acceptance Gates

121. A character traverses slopes, steps, caves and freshly edited terrain without persistent tunnelling,
     floating or wrong-voxel selection.

122. The same edit trace rebuilds render and collision caches without stale generation publication under
     forced scheduling delays.

123. Per-voxel boxes lose the representative collision benchmark to a selected bounded proxy, with
     correctness differences documented.

124. A 30-minute destruction stress scene remains inside configured active-body, collision-cook, memory
     and frame/tick budgets.

125. Disconnected components obey minimum/maximum thresholds and never create more bodies or
     dynamic voxels than the configured event/zone limits.

126. Save/reload preserves authoritative destruction and persistent rigid entities while safely rebuilding
     solver caches.

127. A headless server and graphical client agree on accepted edits and stable entity state despite client
     prediction and correction.

128. Every enabled structural tier has an explicit visual debug view, deterministic scenario tests and a
     statement of what it does not simulate.

129. Jolt admission is recorded in an ADR with version pin, license, adapter surface, deterministic-build
     decision, recook policy and exit strategy.

130. Advanced fluids, heat, FEM or learned physics remain absent unless a later milestone produces
     their own evidence packet.

                                                                                   VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 85
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

Part XI - Networking and MMO Readiness

Part XI authority contract: The dedicated server is the authority for accepted commands, persistent
world edits and gameplay state. The transport is replaceable plumbing; Steam is an optional ecosystem
adapter; and "MMO-ready" is a staged evidence claim, not permission to build distributed services
before one server is correct. Parts VIII-X control coordinates, persistence, scheduling and physics truth.

55. Research Verdict: Build an Authoritative Multiplayer Core, Not an Imaginary
MMO Platform

VoxelForge must eventually support a persistent shared action sandbox, but the budget and team size make
premature MMO infrastructure destructive. The correct Foundation is an in-process authoritative server
with the same protocol boundaries as a remote dedicated server. Only measured connection count, player
density, persistence throughput or geographic latency may justify distribution.

Claim                     Minimum evidence required

Online multiplayer        Remote clients connect through a supported
                          transport and complete reproducible gameplay/edit
                          scenarios.

Dedicated-server capable  Headless server package runs without
                          renderer/editor and owns authoritative state.

Persistent shared world   Committed edits and player/entity state survive
                          restart with crash-recovery evidence.

MMO-ready / MMO-capable   Documented spatial interest, versioned protocols,
                          authoritative persistence, density/load tests and a
                          scale-out boundary experiment.

MMO                       A published concurrency/world-service target is
                          met in a production-like environment with
                          operations, security and cost evidence.

Until the final row is proven, public language must use "persistent multiplayer foundation" or "MMO-
capable architecture," never claim a finished MMO backend.

 Foundation layers may live in one executable and process. A logical boundary does not require a
     microservice.

 The dedicated server builds from the same authoritative modules but excludes renderer, editor, local
     input and nonessential assets.

 All remote input crosses the same validation path used by loopback tests. "Local host" is not a trust
     bypass.

 The transport adapter is selected at runtime/build configuration without changing replication schemas.

56A. Tick, Time and Command Envelope

Field                                          Purpose

Session identity                               Binds the command to an authenticated,
                                               authorized connection.

Client command sequence                        Deduplicates retransmission and detects
                                               gaps/replay.

Client input tick/time sample                  Supports ordering, prediction and latency analysis;
                                               never overrides server time.

Command type and bounded payload               Validated against schema, size, state and rate limits.

Relevant authority versions                    Optional brick/entity baseline for conflict/rejection
                                               feedback.

Trace/correlation ID                           Links client, server, persistence and telemetry
                                               evidence.

 The server assigns the authoritative execution tick/order. Client timestamps are hints and diagnostics,
     not authority.

 Commands are idempotent where practical. Duplicate sequence numbers return the previous result or
     a deterministic duplicate response.

 Every rejection uses a bounded reason code and optional corrective state; it does not expose hidden
     server data.

 Tick rate, physics rate and replication rate are independent configuration values with measured
     budgets.

                                               VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 88
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

57. Authority, Validation and Conflict Rules

 Clients submit intent: movement input, ability use, inventory request or edit tool operation. They never
     submit final transforms, item grants or committed brick bytes as trusted truth.

 The authority validates identity, permissions, reach/raycast, cooldown, inventory/tool capability,
     material/ownership, edit volume, region residency and per-session/global budgets.

 Accepted commands produce deterministic authoritative records. Rejected commands produce no
     partial persistent mutation unless the command type explicitly defines progressive commits.

 Each authority region has a monotonic operation sequence or equivalent total order for conflicting
     persistent writes.

 Cross-region operations are either owned by one coordinator/region or use an explicit transaction
     protocol. Foundation forbids ad-hoc writes to two region authorities.

 Administrative/debug commands use a separate capability, audit trail and build/runtime gate.

57A. Replay, Reconnect and Anti-Duplication

 Session reconnect resumes from a server-issued opaque token and acknowledged protocol state; clients
     cannot choose an arbitrary world revision.

 Persistent edit and economy commands carry stable operation IDs so transport retry cannot duplicate
     effects.

 The server retains a bounded recent-result window or durable idempotency record according to
     consequence severity.

 A reconnect performs baseline/hash negotiation and resynchronizes missing state before enabling new
     consequential commands.

 Replay logs redact secrets and personal data while preserving enough command/order/version evidence
     to reproduce authority bugs.

58. Replication Model: Separate World, Entity and Presentation State

Replication family  Initial state            Incremental state        Correction/resync

Voxel/world bricks  Versioned compressed     Committed edit           Hash/version mismatch
                    brick/region snapshot    operations or compact    requests a bounded
                    plus material/schema     brick deltas with        snapshot; never guess-
                    references.              source/target versions.  merge unknown
                                                                      authority.

Dynamic entities    Spawn record with        Prioritized              Authoritative
                    stable entity ID, type   component/state deltas   snapshot/correction;
                    and baseline             and discrete events.     despawn tombstone.
                    components.

                    and movement config      acknowledgements plus    reconciliation/replay of
                    version.                 server state.            unacknowledged inputs.

                                             VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 89
                  MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

Replication family     Initial state            Incremental state        Correction/resync

Rigid/debris bodies    Only server-owned        Rate/priority-limited    Snap/blend policy;
                       gameplay bodies.         transform, velocity and  client-only visual
                                                state changes.           fragments are not
                                                                         replicated.

                       seed/event.              and most debris          to validate gameplay.
                                                generated locally from
                                                authoritative events.

 A snapshot and delta both declare schema, authority region, object/brick identity, base version and
     resulting version/hash.

 The server keeps only the baselines required by active clients and recovery policy. Baseline memory is
     budgeted per client/region.

 Client caches may be evicted and reconstructed. Eviction never sends a delete to authority unless an
     explicit gameplay command does so.

 Large initial world state is progressive and cancellable. A player may enter a restricted/loading state
     rather than receiving the entire region before any interaction.

58A. Message Classes, Priorities and Head-of-Line Control

Class                  Delivery intent          Examples                 Policy

Control-critical       Reliable, ordered,       Handshake,               Small bounded
                       highest priority.        authentication result,   messages; cannot be
                                                disconnect, protocol     starved by world bulk.
                                                errors.

Authoritative commits  Reliable with explicit   Voxel edit result,       Persist before/with
                       operation IDs.           inventory/economy        acknowledgement
                                                commit,                  according to command
                                                spawn/despawn.           policy.

World bulk             Reliable, chunked,       Brick/region snapshots,  Application chunks
                       cancellable, lower       asset metadata.          allow progress,
                       priority lane.                                    cancellation and
                                                                         resource limits even if
                                                                         transport fragments.
