                       replaceable.             animation state, input   newest valid state wins.
                                                samples.

Telemetry/optional     Low priority and         Debug counters,          Disabled or sampled
                       droppable.               nonessential presence.   under congestion.

GameNetworkingSockets supports reliable and unreliable messages, messages larger than the underlying
MTU, encryption, connection statistics and multiple priority/weighted lanes; it explicitly does not provide

                                                                                   VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 90
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution
higher-level entity serialization, delta encoding or compression. This makes it a transport candidate, not the
network architecture. [R95]

58B. Packetization, MTU and Resource Limits

 The engine defines a conservative normal datagram payload target and records actual transport path
     information where available. It does not assume Ethernet MTU across the Internet.

 Avoid relying on IP fragmentation. RFC 8085 recommends application awareness of datagram size and
     congestion behavior; DPLPMTUD provides a standards path for datagram transports. [R99-R100]

 Transport-level fragmentation is useful, but world snapshots are also application-chunked to support
     cancellation, checksums, decompression bounds, progress and per-chunk retries/resync.

 Every decoder enforces maximum message, field, allocation, nesting, decompressed-size and per-tick
     work limits before allocating/processing.

 Compression is selected per message family. Tiny realtime messages normally remain uncompressed;
     correlated brick snapshots may use the Part VIII codec policy after end-to-end measurement.

 Compressed input is authenticated/validated and decompressed into bounded buffers; compression
     ratios from untrusted peers cannot allocate arbitrary memory.

58C. Schema Evolution and Compatibility

Genesis does not select Protocol Buffers as the universal wire format. Its official compatibility rules are used
as a proven example: stable field identifiers, no reuse of deleted numbers and explicit reserved fields
prevent ambiguous decoding. Any selected schema system or custom codec must provide equivalent
discipline. [R101]

 The handshake negotiates protocol major/minor, feature capabilities, world schema, compression
     options and content compatibility.

 Major mismatch rejects cleanly. Minor evolution may add ignorable fields/messages but cannot silently
     reinterpret an existing field.

 Removed identifiers remain reserved forever inside a protocol family.
 Unknown fields/messages follow an explicit skip/reject policy based on consequence;

     financial/inventory/world mutation messages default to fail closed.
 Network encoding is canonical enough for hashing/testing where required, or the hash is computed

     over a separately defined canonical state representation.
 Golden packet fixtures and cross-version tests are retained in source control.

59. Interest Management Is a Budgeted Subscription System

Broadcasting all state to all players cannot scale and also reveals unnecessary information. Each connection
owns an InterestSet generated from position, camera/gameplay reach, party/ownership, subscribed systems
and server policy. Interest is related to Part VIII residency but is not identical: a server may keep data
resident without replicating it, and a client may need a low-detail summary before detailed bricks.

Priority band  Typical contents                    Update policy

Immediate      Local player, nearby combat,        High rate/low latency; reserve
               accepted edits, collision-relevant  bandwidth.
               doors/platforms.

Interactive    Nearby players/NPCs, visible        Adaptive rate by

               construction, loot and projectiles. distance/relevance.

                                 VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 91
               MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

Priority band  Typical contents                     Update policy

Context        Far structures, settlement state,    Low rate, aggregation or LOD
               weather and landmarks.               summaries.

Background     Guild/economy/global                 Reliable service events; separate
               notifications.                       from spatial tick traffic.

Excluded       Hidden/private or irrelevant         Do not send. Interest
               state.                               management is also data-
                                                    minimization, not merely
                                                    compression.

 Interest calculations are server-owned and deterministic enough to replay/debug. Client camera claims
     cannot force arbitrary subscriptions.

 Per-client byte, message, CPU-encode and pending-reliable budgets are enforced. Priority and distance
     degrade update frequency/detail before critical data is dropped.

 Subscription changes use hysteresis to avoid border thrashing and include generation/version
     baselines.

 Dense hotspots are a first-class benchmark. The engine must test many players editing/building in one
     area, not only players spread evenly across a map.

 Being outside InterestSet is not a complete secrecy control. Sensitive state must be withheld by
     authorization rules even if spatially nearby.

59A. Congestion, Backpressure and Failure Behavior

 A growing reliable queue is an overload signal, not free buffering. The server records queue age/bytes
     and may reduce world bulk, lower update rates, pause interaction or disconnect an unhealthy client by
     explicit policy.

 No client can cause unbounded server memory by refusing to read acknowledgements or repeatedly
     requesting snapshots.

 The network path couples to bounded replication queues, never directly to unbounded world-job
     production.

 Packet loss/latency simulation is mandatory in CI/scenario tests. GameNetworkingSockets provides
     impairment tools and detailed connection statistics useful for this evidence. [R95]

 Server overload applies fair per-session and global budgets; one destructive player cannot starve
     authoritative simulation for everyone.

60. Prediction, Reconciliation and Physics Policy

 Predict local player locomotion and cosmetic response where it materially improves feel. Preserve the
     authoritative server result and an input history for reconciliation.

 Voxel edits may display a temporary preview/ghost, but permanent occupancy/material changes occur
     only after authoritative acceptance. The preview is visually distinguishable under high latency or
     conflict.

 Client prediction never consumes hidden server data and cannot mint items, bypass ownership or
     exceed edit budgets.

 Remote entities interpolate/extrapolate within bounded windows; stale state does not continue
     indefinitely.

                                 VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 92
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

 Most destruction particles and small debris are client-generated presentation. Server-owned detached
     components receive lower-rate authoritative correction.

 Historical lag compensation for combat is a later gameplay ADR. It requires bounded history, anti-abuse
     policy and a definition of whether terrain edits are rewound; it is not silently enabled.

61. Transport and Steam Boundary

GameNetworkingSockets is the leading transport candidate because the open-source library offers
connection-oriented, message-oriented reliable/unreliable delivery over UDP, fragmentation/reassembly,
encryption, latency/loss simulation, statistics, lanes, IPv6 and optional P2P/NAT traversal. It can also operate
without Steam. [R95]

 The Foundation first tests ordinary direct IP/UDP through the open-source library or a minimal
     reference adapter. Steam identity, lobbies and relays remain adapters.

 SteamNetworkingSockets/SDR can relay traffic, hide IP addresses and protect against some DoS
     exposure; the dedicated-server SDR flow can require a game coordinator and signed tickets. [R96-R97]

 The open-source GameNetworkingSockets build does not itself grant access to Valve's relay network.
     SDR availability and cross-store conditions are a partner/service matter. [R95-R97]

 Steam Game Servers APIs help advertise, authenticate and distribute dedicated servers, but Valve states
     that developers host servers themselves or allow the community to host them. Steam is not free general-
     purpose MMO compute hosting. [R98]

 All Steam-specific identifiers are converted to a platform-neutral AccountIdentity at the boundary. The
     engine can later support non-Steam accounts/transports without rewriting world replication.

61A. Dedicated Server Packaging and Hosting Policy

 Produce a headless executable with no GPU requirement, deterministic configuration, structured logs,
     health endpoint/status output and clean shutdown/checkpoint behavior.

 Foundation hosting is local/LAN and one low-cost rented machine only when internet testing is needed.
     No always-on cluster is purchased under the current budget.

 Container packaging is optional for reproducibility; it must not be required for local debugging or hide
     filesystem/persistence behavior.

 Community-hosted servers are a future product decision. Persistent authoritative worlds with
     economy/security may require studio-controlled hosting even if sandbox instances are community-
     hosted.

 Cloud orchestration, Kubernetes, Agones, service mesh and global databases are milestone-gated until a
     single authority process reaches a measured limit.

62. Network Security and Abuse Controls

Threat / failure   Required control

Malformed packets  Length-first parser, bounded allocations, fuzzing,
                   sanitizers and fail-closed decode.

Command cheating   Server recomputation/validation of movement,
                   reach, ownership, tools, cooldowns and edit
                   footprint.

                   VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 93
                  MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

Threat / failure     Required control

Replay/duplication   Session-bound sequences, operation IDs, expiry and
                     idempotency records.

Resource exhaustion  Per-IP/session/account rates, connection caps,
                     snapshot/codec budgets and queue limits.

Identity spoofing    Verified platform/auth ticket or engine account
                     token; never trust a claimed ID in payload.

Information leakage  Authorization-aware interest and minimal error
                     responses; secrets excluded from logs/replays.

DDoS exposure        Transport/relay protection where available,
                     minimal unauthenticated work and
                     infrastructure/provider controls.

Protocol downgrade   Authenticated capability negotiation and minimum
                     supported security/protocol policy.

 Encryption protects transport confidentiality/integrity; it does not make client commands trustworthy.
 Network parsers and persistence decoders share a hostile-input posture. Every remotely reachable

     decoder receives fuzz/property tests.
 Administrative secrets, SDR keys and signing keys never enter the repository, client binary, agent

     context or ordinary logs.
 Moderation/ban/audit features are product-layer policies but must have stable identity and evidence

     hooks in the foundation.

63. Persistence, Region Authority and Scale-Out Rules

 One server process may own many regions. Region is first a persistence/interest key, not a mandatory
     process boundary.

 Persistent commit acknowledgement follows Part VIII durability profiles. A client is not told a
     consequential edit is durable before the selected journal/checkpoint contract is met.

 Region ownership has one writer at a time. A future handoff protocol must transfer authority epoch,
     checkpoint/journal position, active entities, pending idempotency records and client subscriptions.

 During handoff, commands are routed, buffered within strict bounds or rejected/retried. Two
     regions/processes may never both accept the same authority epoch.

 Cross-region player movement is proven before cross-region destruction, vehicles or structures
     spanning boundaries.

 The database/service split is introduced only when contention, durability or operational evidence
     requires it; Foundation uses the simplest crash-consistent storage that satisfies Part VIII.

                     VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 94
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

63A. Scale Test Shapes

Test                                  What it prevents

Many idle connected clients           Mistaking gameplay cost for connection/session
                                      overhead.

Dense crowd in one cell               Hiding worst-case interest and entity replication
                                      behind a large empty world.

Synchronized build/destruction burst  Ignoring edit validation, persistence and brick-delta
                                      amplification.

High-loss/high-latency clients        Reliable queue growth and correction storms.

Reconnect storm after server restart  Authentication, snapshot and storage thundering
                                      herd.

Slow/malicious reader                 Unbounded pending sends and per-client memory.

Long soak with churn                  Leaks in sessions, baselines, entity IDs and
                                      idempotency records.

64. Required Network Observability

 Per server tick: simulation p50/p95/p99/max, command counts/rejections, persistence latency, active
     regions and overload events.

 Per connection: RTT, jitter, packet loss, send/receive rate, reliable queue bytes/age, lane utilization,
     snapshot progress and correction count.

 Per replication family: encoded bytes, compression ratio/time, baseline memory, interest count, stale
     delta rejection and resync causes.

 Per security boundary: auth failures, rate-limit triggers, malformed messages, fuzz regressions and
     administrative actions.

 Metrics cardinality is bounded; player IDs are hashed/redacted where operationally possible.

65. Part XI Acceptance Gates

131. The same authoritative scenario passes through in-process loopback and a real network transport
     without client code writing world state directly.

132. Two remote clients edit the same boundary bricks under latency/loss; the server produces one
     ordered authority history and both clients converge or receive explicit resync.

133. Movement prediction/reconciliation remains playable under the declared test profile and never
     grants persistent authority.

134. Brick snapshot/delta replication survives packet loss, cancellation, stale baselines, reconnect and
     forced hash mismatch without silent divergence.

135. All remotely reachable decoders pass size-limit, malformed-input, sanitizer and fuzz/property tests.
136. A slow client cannot cause unbounded reliable queues, snapshot memory or server job production.
137. The headless server restarts from crash-consistent persistence, reconnects clients and prevents

     duplicate consequential operations.

                                      VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 95
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution
138. GameNetworkingSockets or any chosen transport is pinned behind an adapter and benchmarked

     against a minimal reference for latency, throughput, CPU, memory and failure diagnostics.
139. Steam integration proves authentication/discovery/relay behavior separately and documentation

     states clearly that Steam does not supply the project's game-server compute hosting.
140. A dense hotspot load test publishes player/entity/edit counts, p95/p99 tick time, outbound

     bandwidth and the first measured scale limit before any "MMO" claim.
141. No gateway, coordinator, microservice, distributed database or orchestration platform is added

     without a failing single-process measurement and an ADR.
142. Protocol evolution tests verify at least one supported older/newer minor pair and reject an

     incompatible major version cleanly.

65A. Hermes Directives for Parts X and XI

 Do not implement structural stress, networking scale-out or Steam services before the preceding
     maturity gate is proven.

 For each physics or networking dependency, pin an exact version/commit, capture license/provenance,
     build a minimal adapter and write an exit/migration note.

 Every benchmark includes a simple baseline, representative voxel/edit workload, failure injection and
     raw artifacts.

 Treat all network input and imported physics/world data as hostile until validated and bounded.
 Update PROJECT_STATE.md with current maturity tier, measured limits, unresolved risks and the exact

     next falsifiable experiment.
 When research conflicts with Genesis, write an ADR proposal; do not silently replace authority,

     persistence, coordinate or threading contracts.

Part XII - Editor, Tooling and Asset Pipeline

  Part XII authority contract: The editor and every asset tool are clients of the same engine-facing
  commands, validation rules and persistent formats used by runtime and server code. They may expose
  more diagnostics and permissions, but they may not mutate World Authority, Asset Authority or project
  state through hidden backdoors. Source files are human-owned inputs; cooked artifacts and preview
  caches are reproducible derivatives.

66. Research Verdict: Build a Diagnostic Workbench Before a General-Purpose
Editor

VoxelForge needs excellent tooling to reach the tactile, readable and highly editable fantasy sandbox shown
by the founder moodboard. It does not need an Unreal-sized editor before the engine has stable authority,
streaming, rendering and edit contracts. The first product is a diagnostic workbench embedded in the
viewer: it makes invisible engine state visible, executes validated commands and records evidence. Dear
ImGui is well suited to programmer-facing visualization and content tools, while its own documentation
explicitly notes missing high-level capabilities such as full internationalization and accessibility. It is
therefore an excellent Foundation tool layer, not a constitutional promise for the final public editor. [R103]

                                                                                   VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 96

                          benchmark commands.       machine-readable           clicking in CI.

66A. Editor Application Boundary

 The editor is an application assembled from engine modules. Core runtime/server libraries never
     depend on editor UI code.

 All consequential actions become typed Commands or EditTransactions. The editor may construct,
     preview and submit them; authority validates and commits them.

 Inspectors consume read-only snapshots, stable handles or query APIs. Debug-only introspection may
     expose extra data but must be compiled and permission-gated.

 Editor state - layout, open panels, camera bookmarks and user preferences - is separate from
     project/world state and cannot dirty authoritative content accidentally.

 The editor must be operable on the founder workstation, but headless tools remain the source of truth
     for CI, cooking and reproducible builds.

 No editor convenience is allowed to bypass Parts VIII-XI durability, coordinate, concurrency, physics or
     networking rules.

                                                    VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 97
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

67. Interaction Model: Commands, Preview, Commit and Undo

A voxel-native editor handles potentially enormous edits, asynchronous derived work and persistent
worlds. A generic object-property undo stack is insufficient. VoxelForge uses explicit transaction semantics
that align with the journal and replication model.

143. Begin a command with stable command type, author/session, selection/frame version and bounded
     parameters.

144. Generate a preview against an immutable source snapshot. Preview meshes, highlights and
     estimates are presentation-only.

145. Validate permissions, affected bounds, material rules, memory/work estimates and source
     generations.

146. Commit one canonical EditTransaction through World Authority; assign transaction/order
     identifiers.

147. Schedule derived mesh, collision, LOD, persistence and network work from the committed
     generation.

148. Record an inverse operation or compact before-image sufficient for a declared undo window. Never
     assume every procedural tool is exactly invertible.

149. Undo is a new authorized transaction, not history deletion. Collaborative or server worlds may
     reject an undo if later conflicting edits make the inverse unsafe.

150. Checkpoint or compact old history according to Part VIII; an editor session cannot retain unlimited
     voxel diffs in RAM.

 Brush tools report estimated affected voxels/bricks, memory, journal bytes and derived workload
     before large commits.

 Long operations support cancellation before commit; after commit, cancellation stops optional derived
     work but cannot pretend the authoritative edit never happened.

 Selection uses stable world coordinates and IDs, never raw pointers or frame-local GPU indices.
 Gizmos operate in Part VIII local frames and display global coordinates separately to avoid large-world

     precision leaks.

68. Asset Authority and Identity

  Asset rule: A pathname is a locator, not permanent identity. A source file is not a runtime resource. A
  cooked blob is not authoring truth. Each layer has an explicit role and version.

 AssetId is a stable logical identifier stored in project manifests and references. It survives source-file
     moves and is never a process pointer or array index.

 SourceIdentity records canonical project-relative path/URI, source content hash, importer recipe,
     importer/tool version and declared options.

 ArtifactKey is derived from all inputs that affect output: source/dependency hashes, importer version,
     target capability profile, build configuration and format/schema versions.

 Cooked artifacts are immutable. A change produces a new key/blob; publication atomically changes the
     logical AssetId mapping after validation.

 The Asset Registry records type, schema, source, dependencies, variants, licenses/provenance, artifact
     keys, diagnostics and last successful cook.

 Missing or failed assets resolve to explicit typed placeholders and diagnostics. They do not silently reuse
     stale data unless policy identifies that exact fallback.

OpenUSD demonstrates useful separation between logical asset references and physical storage through its
Asset Resolver, and its composition/layering model supports non-destructive authoring and working-set
management. Genesis adopts those principles selectively; it does not adopt USD as VoxelForge runtime
authority. [R104-R105]

69. Deterministic Import and Cook Graph

The asset pipeline is a dependency graph, not a folder watcher that runs arbitrary converters. Every node
declares typed inputs, outputs, version, target profile, resource requirements and diagnostics. Identical
declared inputs must produce byte-identical output where practical; where a third-party tool cannot
guarantee this, the manifest records the non-deterministic boundary and semantic validation replaces a
false reproducibility claim.

151. Discover source and recipe; reject paths escaping approved roots or unsupported schemes.
152. Hash source bytes and normalized recipe; resolve and hash declared dependencies through the

     Asset Resolver.
153. Validate structure, sizes, counts, encoding and license/provenance metadata before expensive

     processing.
154. Import into a narrow normalized intermediate representation with explicit units, axes, color space

     and coordinate conventions.
155. Transform/optimize deterministically under a pinned toolchain and target capability profile.
156. Validate cooked structure and semantic invariants; optionally load in a minimal test runtime.
157. Write artifact to a temporary path, fsync/close as required, then atomically publish by ArtifactKey.
158. Update registry mapping only after all outputs and dependency records succeed; failed cooks

     preserve the previous known-good mapping.
159. Emit machine-readable report with timings, warnings, hashes, versions, peak memory and output

     sizes.

 File timestamps are hints for discovery only. They never replace content/dependency hashes.
 Parallel cooking respects bounded CPU, RAM, GPU and I/O slots. CTest-style abstract resource allocation

     is a model for scheduling scarce test/tool resources. [R110]
 Build graph cycles are rejected with an explanatory dependency path.

                                                                                 VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 100
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution
 A cache hit is verified by schema/key and optional artifact checksum before use.
 Cook correctness tests compare clean build, incremental build, moved source, changed dependency,

     changed tool version and deleted cache.

glTF explicitly positions itself as an API-neutral runtime asset delivery format complementary to heavier
authoring formats. VoxelForge therefore treats it as a disciplined interchange/distillation input, not as the
engine scene or asset database. KTX 2.0 is similarly an efficient GPU texture container with mip streaming
and universal/supercompressed options, but actual GPU support and transcode targets remain capability-
dependent. [R106,R108]

                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

70A. Voxel Material and Visual-North-Star Pipeline

 Voxel materials separate simulation identity from visual layers. MaterialId and physical/gameplay
     properties remain stable while texture/detail variants may be recooked.

 The palette records albedo/value range, roughness, normal/detail policy, emissive parameters, biome
     variation and accessibility/readability checks.

 The founder moodboard is encoded as testable art-direction constraints: warm earth/wood/stone
     values, readable faceted silhouettes, saturated but controlled foliage, atmospheric depth, strong contact
     grounding and legible magical emission.

 These constraints produce reference scenes and material swatches, not copyrighted asset extraction or
     a mandate to duplicate another game.

 Macro voxel form must remain readable without high-frequency textures. Texture detail supports form
     rather than hiding weak geometry.

 Conventional meshes remain valid for characters, cloth-like elements and props when voxel authority
     provides no benefit; their material response must still fit the shared world palette.

71. Shader, Pipeline and Hot-Reload Policy

 Source shaders compile offline through the pinned Part V path. Cooked SPIR-V and generated
     reflection/interface metadata are the runtime inputs.

 A shader interface hash covers descriptor bindings, push constants, specialization constants,
     vertex/mesh inputs and required capabilities.

 Hot reload compiles and validates in a worker process/job, creates new GPU objects off the live path and
     publishes atomically at a frame-safe point.

 Compile or pipeline failure retains the last known-good object and displays the full diagnostic; it never
     replaces a working shader with null/undefined state.

 Interface-compatible reload may replace in place. Interface-breaking changes require dependent
     material/pipeline recook or explicit editor restart/migration.

 Runtime-generated shader permutations are bounded by declared features. A combinatorial
     permutation explosion is a pipeline bug, not accepted flexibility.

 Pipeline caches are acceleration data tied to device/driver compatibility and are never authority or
     source-controlled universal binaries.

71A. Hot Reload Beyond Shaders

Asset type           Reload approach                   Publication safety

                     new GPU resources; update         frame/descriptor can reference it.
                     generation-checked handle.

                     compatibility; stage buffers and  dependents explicitly.
                     derived collision separately.

                     migration impact; world           reinterpret old IDs silently.

72. Tool UX, Automation and Security

 Every production tool supports --help, deterministic noninteractive mode, meaningful exit codes and
     JSON (or equally structured) diagnostics.

 Interactive editor actions expose the equivalent command/transaction in logs or a reproducible recipe
     where practical.

 Long-running tools report phase, progress, resource use and cancellation status; a frozen window is a
     defect.

 Imported files and metadata are untrusted. Parsers enforce file/element/string/decompression/output
     limits and run with least privilege; high-risk third-party converters run in a constrained
     process/container.

 Importer plugins cannot execute arbitrary scripts embedded in content. Extensions are allow-listed and
     unknown consequential extensions fail closed.

 Tool crashes cannot corrupt the previous known-good registry/artifact mapping. Temporary outputs are
     isolated and cleaned/recovered explicitly.

 No native plugin ABI, reflection framework, node editor or scripting language is added until at least two
     concrete workflows need it and an ADR compares process isolation, data protocol and ABI/API options.

72A. Part XII Acceptance Gates

160. The same voxel edit can be issued from a headless command and the editor, producing the same
     canonical transaction and world hash.

161. Undo/redo survives brick boundaries, asynchronous derived rebuilds, save/reload and a configured
     bounded history window; conflicting collaborative undo fails explicitly.

162. The diagnostic workbench exposes every state required to explain one streaming, rendering,
     physics, persistence and network failure scenario.

163. A clean cook and an incremental cook of the same representative asset corpus produce identical
     accepted artifact keys and semantic outputs.

164. Moving/renaming a source does not break references when AssetId is unchanged; changing any
     declared dependency invalidates exactly the correct artifacts.

165. Representative glTF assets pass the Khronos validator and VoxelForge importer limits; unsupported
     extensions produce actionable failure rather than partial silent import.

166. KTX 2.0 candidates are evaluated for quality, file size, transcode/decode time, upload time and
     target-device support before a default is selected.

167. Hot reload retains the last known-good resource on compile/cook/upload failure and never exposes
     a destroyed GPU/resource handle.

168. Deleting the derived-data cache and rebuilding from tracked source/recipes yields a working
     project.

169. Malformed/decompression-bomb/path-traversal import cases are bounded and covered by
     sanitizers/fuzz tests under Part XIII.

                                                                                 VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 103
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

170. No editor-only data structure becomes a second world, asset, physics or render authority.
171. A real creator workflow - sculpt/build, material variation, save, reload and package - is timed and its

     top friction points are recorded before E3 expansion.

72B. Hermes Directives for Part XII

 Implement E0/E1 before E2, and do not begin a polished editor shell while core diagnostic state remains
     invisible.

 Prefer small command-line tools and direct schemas over reflection, generic node graphs or plugin
     systems.

 Pin every importer/compiler/tool version and record its license, provenance, options and output in the
     artifact manifest.

 For every new asset class, define source, normalized intermediate, cooked format, validator, size limits,
     dependency rules, hot-reload behavior and migration policy before code merges.

 Use official format validators where available, but add VoxelForge-specific semantic limits and tests;
     standards compliance alone is not product correctness.

 Update PROJECT_STATE.md with editor tier, supported asset corpus, cook reproducibility status, known
     non-determinism and next creator workflow experiment.

Part XIII - Testing, Profiling and Performance Gates

Part XIII authority contract: No subsystem may declare itself correct, fast, scalable or production-ready
using weaker evidence than this Part. Correctness gates precede performance gates. Benchmarks are
versioned experiments with raw artifacts, not screenshots of one favorable run. Instrumented diagnostic
builds and shipping-performance builds answer different questions and must never be compared as if
equivalent.

73. Research Verdict: Evidence Must Be Layered, Reproducible and Falsifiable

VoxelForge combines persistence, mutable spatial data, asynchronous CPU/GPU work and eventual
networking. A green unit-test suite alone cannot establish correctness, and average FPS cannot establish
performance. The evidence system therefore combines isolated tests, generative/fuzz tests, replay/state-
hash tests, integration and fault injection, GPU/API validation, visual reference tests, long soaks and
controlled benchmarks. GoogleTest emphasizes independent, repeatable, portable tests; CTest supplies
repository-defined presets and resource-aware scheduling; these become the Foundation harness rather
than ad-hoc executable scripts. [R110-R112]

74. Test Design Rules

 Tests are independent, repeatable and runnable in isolation. Test order, wall-clock date, user profile
     and previous cache state cannot decide pass/fail unless explicitly part of the scenario. [R112]

 Each defect receives the smallest durable regression test at the lowest useful layer, plus a higher-level
     test when the failure crossed contracts.

 Tests assert invariants and observable contracts, not private implementation shape. A refactor should
     not require rewriting unrelated expected internals.

 Random tests record seed, generator version and minimized failing input. "Could not reproduce" is
     unacceptable when the seed/artifact exists.

 Timeout, memory and output limits are part of parser and async tests. A non-crashing infinite/hyper-
     expensive input is still a failure.

 Golden binary formats include schema/version and semantic readers; byte equality is required only
     where canonical encoding is a contract.

 Floating-point and image tests use justified tolerances and invariant checks. Broad tolerances that hide
     real regressions are forbidden.

 Mocks are used only at real boundaries. Prefer small fakes/reference implementations for storage,
     clocks, transport and GPU-independent algorithms.

74A. Property, Fuzz and Hostile-Input Program

libFuzzer is an in-process coverage-guided engine that repeatedly mutates a corpus and is most effective
with narrow, deterministic and fast targets. LLVM notes that active feature work has moved elsewhere
while libFuzzer remains supported for important fixes; Genesis therefore treats the fuzz target
interface/corpus as durable and the exact engine as replaceable. [R114]

 Initial targets: brick/journal/manifest/wire decoders, glTF/KTX-facing wrappers, compression
     boundaries, shader reflection metadata and edit-command parsing.

 Each target accepts empty, truncated, huge-declared, malformed and semantically inconsistent input
     without unbounded allocation, hang, process exit or external side effect.

 Fuzz builds combine coverage guidance with ASan/UBSan where supported. Threaded targets join all
     work and clear global state between iterations.

 Corpora contain valid small examples, known historical failures, boundary values and hand-built
     invalid structures. Crashes are minimized and checked into regression corpus when legally safe.

 Property tests cover round-trip, canonicalization, idempotency, monotonic versions, no lost edit across
     brick boundaries, and equivalent results across alternate execution partitions.

 Fuzz/soak runs emit engine revision, compiler, sanitizer, seed/corpus hash, duration, executions,
     coverage summary and artifact path.

                               VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 106
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

     narrow scope and expiry/review date.

 A validation mode that cannot run because of an extension/model conflict is recorded as a coverage
     gap, not silently skipped.

76. Replay, State Hashing and Visual Regression

 Canonical state hashes cover authoritative world/entity/persistence state only. Derived GPU meshes,
     caches, pointer values and unordered container iteration are excluded or normalized.

                                                     VoxelForge / Land of the Voxel Engine - 16 July 2026 Page 107
                         MICRO-VOXEL GENESIS OS v2.2 | Autonomous Engineering Constitution

 Replay artifacts contain engine/schema versions, seed, initial checkpoint hash, command/event stream
     and expected checkpoints/invariants.

 When cross-platform floating behavior cannot be bit-identical, tests compare domain invariants and
     explicitly quantized/canonical outputs rather than pretending full determinism.

 Visual tests capture final color plus useful semantic buffers: depth, normals/material IDs, motion,
     object/brick IDs, LOD and debug masks.

 Pixel-perfect comparison is reserved for the same controlled software/hardware path. Cross-GPU tests
     use perceptual/structural thresholds, masks and human review for intentional art changes.

 Every approved visual baseline includes scene revision, camera, exposure, time/weather seed, render
     profile, resolution, GPU/driver and a review note.

 The Lay of the Land-inspired north-star scene is a visual target suite, not the sole performance scene;
     beauty and stress workloads remain separate.

77. Performance Evidence Contract

  Performance claim rule: A claim is valid only for the named engine revision, build, scene/workload,
  hardware, driver/OS, settings, sample protocol and metric. Any omitted dimension makes the claim
  exploratory, not a gate.

 Correctness and validation gates pass before a result can promote architecture. A faster wrong output is
     a failed experiment.

 Benchmarks compare against the simplest credible baseline and, where relevant, at least one
     alternative architecture.

 Warm-up, repetitions and machine-readable output are mandatory. Google Benchmark explicitly
     supports warm-up time, repetitions, random interleaving, context and JSON output; VoxelForge uses
     those capabilities for microbenchmarks rather than copying one-shot console numbers. [R113]

 CPU microbenchmarks isolate setup from measured work, prevent dead-code elimination and report
     throughput/items/bytes where meaningful.

 End-to-end frame/tick benchmarks report distributions and spikes: p50, p90, p95, p99, p99.9 where
     sample count justifies it, maximum, missed-budget count and hitch duration.

 Changes below the declared noise floor are "no demonstrated difference," not wins. The benchmark
     record includes variability and raw samples.

 GPU and CPU clocks/power modes, background load, thermal state and capture/instrumentation status
     are recorded. Results from unstable throttling are rejected.

 No benchmark is run from an IDE debug session or sanitizer/validation build unless the question
     specifically concerns that configuration.

 Instrumentation has measured overhead budgets and can be sampled/disabled. An observer that
     changes the workload materially is documented.

 Counters have units, ownership, reset/window semantics and bounded cardinality. A graph without
     defined units is not evidence.

 CPU/GPU events share frame/tick/transaction IDs where possible so one voxel edit can be followed
     through authority, jobs, upload, rendering, collision and persistence.

 Profiler integrations remain adapters. Engine code does not require one proprietary tool to function or
     diagnose basic failures.
